				<title><![CDATA[[Interview] [Galaxy Unpacked July 2026] Inside the Design Behind the Latest Galaxy Z Series and Galaxy Watch]]></title>
				<link>https://news.samsung.com/global/interview-galaxy-unpacked-july-2026-inside-the-design-behind-the-latest-galaxy-z-series-and-galaxy-watch</link>
				<pubDate>Mon, 03 Aug 2026 13:00:00 +0000</pubDate>
								<media:content url="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/03102759/Samsung-Mobile-Galaxy-Unpacked-July-2026-Inside-the-Design-Galaxy-Z-Series-Galaxy-Watch_Thumb932-728x410.jpg" medium="image" />
				<dc:creator><![CDATA[Samsung Newsroom]]></dc:creator>

		<category><![CDATA[Galaxy Z Fold8 Ultra]]></category>

									<description><![CDATA[On July 22 at Galaxy Unpacked July 2026, Samsung Electronics introduced Galaxy Z Fold8 Ultra, Galaxy Z Fold8, Galaxy Z Flip8, Galaxy Watch Ultra2 and Galaxy Watch9. From foldables to smartwatches, Samsung’s latest Galaxy devices take different forms but share the same design philosophy — comfort. Samsung Newsroom spoke with Hubert Lee, Executive Vice President […]]]></description>
																<content:encoded><![CDATA[
<p>On July 22 at Galaxy Unpacked July 2026, Samsung Electronics introduced Galaxy Z Fold8 Ultra, Galaxy Z Fold8, Galaxy Z Flip8, Galaxy Watch Ultra2 and Galaxy Watch9.</p>



<p>From foldables to smartwatches, Samsung’s latest Galaxy devices take different forms but share the same design philosophy — comfort.</p>



<p>Samsung Newsroom spoke with Hubert Lee, Executive Vice President and Head of the Design Team, Mobile eXperience (MX) Business at Samsung Electronics, to hear how this approach shaped the new lineup.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="1000" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/03105020/Samsung-Mobile-Galaxy-Unpacked-July-2026-Inside-the-Design-Galaxy-Z-Series-Galaxy-Watch_Main1.jpg" alt="Hubert Lee" class="wp-image-176129" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ Hubert Lee</figcaption></figure>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Q. What were the key design considerations for this year’s Galaxy foldable lineup?</strong></h2>



<p>Today’s Galaxy devices are faster and more powerful than ever. We considered how we could go one step further to deliver a more natural and comfortable experience.</p>



<p>Slimness and lightness are essential to creating a comfortable user experience, since they influence how naturally a device opens, how comfortable it feels in the hand and how easily it fits into everyday life. That is why we consider not only the product itself, but also every moment of the user experience it creates.</p>



<p>From an aesthetic perspective, we applied the concept of “Emotive Geometry.” This design approach brings human warmth to Galaxy’s simple, refined forms, creating a balanced design that goes beyond powerful performance to connect with users on an emotional level.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="667" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/03095648/Samsung-Mobile-Galaxy-Unpacked-July-2026-Inside-the-Design-Galaxy-Z-Series-Galaxy-Watch_Main2.jpg" alt="Product sketches and design elements on display illustrate the design process behind the new Galaxy Z series." class="wp-image-176109" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ Product sketches and design elements on display illustrate the design process behind the new Galaxy Z series.</figcaption></figure>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Q. What drove the expansion of the Galaxy Z series portfolio?</strong></h2>



<p>Across seven generations of foldable innovation, we’ve gained a deeper understanding of how people use their devices. As user expectations for foldables have become more diverse, we have also recognized that no single foldable can meet every need – One size does not fit all.</p>



<p>Some users prioritize productivity and multitasking, while others value portability. Based on these insights, we expanded the Galaxy Z series lineup so each device could better serve different priorities.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="750" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/03100019/Samsung-Mobile-Galaxy-Unpacked-July-2026-Inside-the-Design-Galaxy-Z-Series-Galaxy-Watch_Main3.jpg" alt="An image of Galaxy Z Series" class="wp-image-176110" /></figure>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Q. Galaxy Z Fold8 Ultra is the slimmest Galaxy Z Fold yet. What shaped its exceptionally slim design?</strong></h2>



<p>The design concept behind the newest Galaxy Z series is “Emotive Slim” — an approach that focuses not only on reducing thickness, but also on how slim it looks and feels in everyday use.</p>



<p>We applied a gentle slope to the frame to make the foldable easier to open and created a more curved outer silhouette for a softer, more comfortable grip. We also minimized the flat side profile to create a visually slimmer appearance.</p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading">Q. Why was a passport chosen as the design inspiration for the new Galaxy Z Fold8?</h2>



<p>The classic passport is designed to be easy to carry, hold with one hand and read at a glance. Those same ergonomic principles shaped Galaxy Z Fold8, extending our focus on comfort throughout the device’s overall layout.</p>



<p>When folded, it features a 16:10 aspect ratio optimized for one-handed use. Unfolded, it becomes a more immersive 4:3 display with balanced symmetry.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-large"><img loading="lazy" width="768" height="1024" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/03100645/Samsung-Mobile-Galaxy-Unpacked-July-2026-Inside-the-Design-Galaxy-Z-Series-Galaxy-Watch_Main4-768x1024.jpg" alt="An image of Galaxy Z Fold8 in lavender" class="wp-image-176111" /></figure>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Q. How were these colors chosen for the new Galaxy Z series?</strong></h2>



<p>Each color was chosen to reflect the lifestyle of the people each device is designed for. Violet Shadow gives Galaxy Z Fold8 Ultra a chic, sophisticated look, while Lavender brings freshness and softness to Galaxy Z Fold8. Pink adds vibrant, playful energy to Galaxy Z Flip8. Cream and Graphite round out the palette with timeless versatility.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="1086" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/03100748/Samsung-Mobile-Galaxy-Unpacked-July-2026-Inside-the-Design-Galaxy-Z-Series-Galaxy-Watch_Main5.jpg" alt="An image of a color palette" class="wp-image-176112" /></figure>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Q. How did Samsung refine the design of Galaxy Watch Ultra2 and Galaxy Watch9?</strong></h2>



<p>If a smartphone lives in the hand, a smartwatch delivers its greatest value when it’s comfortable enough to wear every day. That’s why our approach to the new Galaxy Watch series focused on refinement rather than reinvention, preserving our core design while enhancing the details that matter most.</p>



<p>At the center of Galaxy Watch is the cushion design. More than a shape, it defines the watch’s identity by balancing presence with softness and precision with comfort. For Galaxy Watch Ultra2, we brought the screen, bezel and cushion body into a seamless form, creating a cleaner profile and a more premium look. We also carefully refined the spacing and tactile separation between the buttons, making each one easier to distinguish by touch and enabling precise control without looking at the screen.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="750" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/03101005/Samsung-Mobile-Galaxy-Unpacked-July-2026-Inside-the-Design-Galaxy-Z-Series-Galaxy-Watch_Main6.jpg" alt="An image of Galaxy Watch Ultra2 and Galaxy Watch9" class="wp-image-176113" /></figure>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Q. How have the bands on Galaxy Watch Ultra2 and Galaxy Watch9 changed?</strong></h2>



<p>Bands play a critical role in wearability. For this generation, we made them lighter, thinner and more breathable with a new air pocket structure.</p>



<p>Because every wrist is different, we used computational design to validate comfort and fit using more than one hundred million real-user wrist data and ten thousand of simulations. We refined the balance between the watch body and band for greater stability on the wrist, while expanded band options give users more ways to express their personal style.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="750" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/03101241/Samsung-Mobile-Galaxy-Unpacked-July-2026-Inside-the-Design-Galaxy-Z-Series-Galaxy-Watch_Main7.jpg" alt="The Latest Galaxy Watch" class="wp-image-176114" /></figure>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Q. What design philosophy ties this year’s Galaxy devices together?</strong></h2>



<p>In an era shaped by AI-driven change, human-centered values are more important than ever. Design shouldn’t only be seen — it should also be felt. For design to feel truly human, it must begin with a deep understanding of how people live, work and interact with technology every day. That belief has guided our approach to designing foldables and smartwatches.</p>
]]></content:encoded>

					<item>
				<title><![CDATA[[Interview] [Galaxy Unpacked July 2026] Intelligent Eyewear: The First Step Toward the Next Mobile AI Interface]]></title>
				<link>https://news.samsung.com/global/interview-galaxy-unpacked-july-2026-intelligent-eyewear-the-first-step-toward-the-next-mobile-ai-interface</link>
				<pubDate>Fri, 31 Jul 2026 08:00:00 +0000</pubDate>
								<media:content url="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/30160657/Samsung-Mobile-Galaxy-Unpacked-July-2026-Intelligent-Eyewear-Interview_Thumb932-728x410.jpg" medium="image" />
				<dc:creator><![CDATA[Samsung Newsroom]]></dc:creator>
						<category><![CDATA[Mobile]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Galaxy Unpacked July 2026]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Intelligent Eyewear]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Interview]]></category>
                <guid isPermaLink="false">https://bit.ly/3RyIR4p</guid>
									<description><![CDATA[On July 22 at Galaxy Unpacked July 2026, Samsung Electronics introduced intelligent eyewear as the first step toward making AI glasses the next interface for mobile AI. Built on decades of expertise in mobile engineering, hardware design and product craftsmanship, intelligent eyewear combines true wearability with mobile AI experiences and seamless connectivity within the Galaxy […]]]></description>
																<content:encoded><![CDATA[
<p>On July 22 at Galaxy Unpacked July 2026, Samsung Electronics introduced intelligent eyewear<sup data-fn="de9248b9-087b-4a39-9ef5-5180cd45b3e5" class="fn"><a href="#de9248b9-087b-4a39-9ef5-5180cd45b3e5" id="de9248b9-087b-4a39-9ef5-5180cd45b3e5-link">1</a></sup> as the first step toward making AI glasses the next interface for mobile AI.</p>



<p>Built on decades of expertise in mobile engineering, hardware design and product craftsmanship, intelligent eyewear combines true wearability with mobile AI experiences and seamless connectivity within the Galaxy ecosystem.</p>



<p>Samsung Newsroom spoke with James Choi, Executive Vice President and Head of XR R&D Team, Mobile eXperience (MX) Business at Samsung Electronics, to learn about the development of intelligent eyewear, the engineering behind its design and Samsung’s long-term vision for the AI glasses category.</p>


<div class="wp-block-image">
<figure class="aligncenter size-large"><img loading="lazy" width="683" height="1024" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/30151938/Samsung-Mobile-Galaxy-Unpacked-July-2026-Intelligent-Eyewear-Interview_main1-683x1024.jpg" alt="Samsung Newsroom spoke with James Choi, Executive Vice President and Head of XR R&D Team, Mobile eXperience (MX) Business at Samsung Electronics." class="wp-image-176076" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ James Choi</figcaption></figure></div>


<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Q. Why is Samsung introducing intelligent eyewear now and why does it believe AI glasses will become the next interface for mobile AI?</strong></h2>



<p>AI glasses will only become mainstream if people genuinely want to wear them every day. Achieving this level of wearability requires more than advanced AI — it also demands deep engineering expertise, mobile technologies and an understanding of how people use technology in their daily lives. Drawing on decades of mobile leadership, Samsung is well positioned to bring these elements together and help move the category forward.</p>



<p>AI glasses understand what users see, hear and experience around them and enable more natural, contextual and seamless interactions, bringing AI closer to the moments that matter in everyday life. Our goal is not to replace the mobile phone but to create AI glasses that help people use their phones less, while getting more value from the mobile experiences they rely on. Samsung is laying the foundation for this vision through the new intelligent eyewear, extending mobile AI beyond the screen.<strong></strong></p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Q. What are the biggest engineering priorities when developing AI glasses?</strong></h2>



<p>The journey began with Galaxy XR. Through our experience developing it, we learned how to design technology that people can comfortably wear on their faces. We applied those insights to intelligent eyewear, focusing every engineering decision on comfort, fit and everyday use.</p>



<p>Wearability isn’t simply a design challenge — it’s an engineering one. When it comes to eyewear, even a tenth of a millimeter matters. That is why we took a holistic approach, carefully considering weight, materials, thermal management and battery efficiency.</p>



<p>Designed to be slim and light, intelligent eyewear incorporates patented slim hinges, advanced thin-wall molding, lightweight materials and optimized hardware components. Its hinge structure prevents corrosion and withstands the demands of everyday wear, while the patented temple architecture improves structural rigidity. Thermal graphite material on the left side of the glasses also helps prevent heat generated by the AP (Application Processor) from transferring to the user’s face.</p>



<p>Because every face is different, we used computational design and worked closely with our eyewear partners to refine the fit across key contact points including the forehead, cheekbones and behind the ears. We also balanced the weight across both sides of the frame, so users can barely notice any difference.</p>



<p>Samsung has filed around 200 patent applications in this category, reflecting its long-term investment in intelligent eyewear. Ultimately, all that engineering is designed to disappear into the experience, making the glasses feel as natural as an ordinary pair.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="750" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/30152125/Samsung-Mobile-Galaxy-Unpacked-July-2026-Intelligent-Eyewear-Interview_main2.jpg" alt="Intelligent eyewear was introduced at Galaxy Unpacked July 2026." class="wp-image-176077" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ Intelligent eyewear was introduced at Galaxy Unpacked July 2026.</figcaption></figure>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Q. How did you ensure intelligent eyewear was resilient enough for everyday use?</strong></h2>



<p>Users expect Samsung-quality products, regardless of form factor. We applied the manufacturing expertise and rigorous verification standards developed over decades of Galaxy device innovation, while introducing additional testing tailored to intelligent eyewear.</p>



<p>Because it is worn directly on the face, intelligent eyewear had to meet the standards of both mobile devices and eyewear — and go beyond them. It underwent extensive durability, environmental and safety testing, including exposure to high temperatures, humidity, sunscreen, cosmetics and sweat, as well as folding and hinge expansion tests.</p>



<p>Despite the extensive engineering packed into its slim and lightweight design, intelligent eyewear delivers uncompromising performance, including strong battery life. Through hardware and software optimization, intelligent eyewear provides up to nine hours of active use on a single charge, even during demanding experiences such as Gemini and video recording. The charging case enables more than seven full recharges for extended multi-day use.<sup data-fn="a82096eb-35f9-44f6-8f3a-0d68160d1721" class="fn"><a href="#a82096eb-35f9-44f6-8f3a-0d68160d1721" id="a82096eb-35f9-44f6-8f3a-0d68160d1721-link">2</a></sup></p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Q. Why do you see intelligent eyewear as a new entry point for mobile AI?</strong></h2>



<p>Intelligent eyewear introduces a more natural and intuitive way to experience mobile AI. Its ability to understand what users see, hear and experience around them enables more personalized, contextual support in the moment.</p>



<p>With a high-quality camera, microphones and speakers integrated into its frame, intelligent eyewear responds through natural conversation, while reducing the need for users to stop and reach for a phone.</p>



<p>From real-time guidance and live translation to line-of-sight capture and context-aware assistance, these experiences are designed to help users access mobile AI while staying present in what they are doing.<sup data-fn="85bbf040-be60-4baf-81a9-b8f0e05528d2" class="fn"><a href="#85bbf040-be60-4baf-81a9-b8f0e05528d2" id="85bbf040-be60-4baf-81a9-b8f0e05528d2-link">3</a></sup></p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Q. How does intelligent eyewear fit within the broader Galaxy ecosystem?</strong></h2>



<p>Intelligent eyewear is designed to work as a companion device to Galaxy phones and other Galaxy wearables, extending mobile AI beyond the screen through more personalized experiences. Users can access apps and services they already rely on — from Samsung and Google apps to third-party content — through a natural, hands-free interface.</p>



<p>For example, they can start recording hands-free with intelligent eyewear, preview what they have captured on Now Bar and then access it in Galaxy Gallery on their phone.<sup data-fn="a9c05d96-0632-4c36-9304-5716721d8e92" class="fn"><a href="#a9c05d96-0632-4c36-9304-5716721d8e92" id="a9c05d96-0632-4c36-9304-5716721d8e92-link">4</a></sup> Through split computing, processing is distributed between intelligent eyewear and the connected Galaxy phone, helping optimize performance.</p>



<p>Intelligent eyewear also works with Galaxy Watch and Galaxy Buds. Users can manage the device directly from their wrist through the Galaxy Watch screen or with intuitive gestures.<sup data-fn="c239e622-8346-4a82-aa25-f295340f416f" class="fn"><a href="#c239e622-8346-4a82-aa25-f295340f416f" id="c239e622-8346-4a82-aa25-f295340f416f-link">5</a></sup> When Galaxy Buds are connected, they can switch audio without interrupting the experience.</p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Q. How did you integrate privacy and trust into the design of intelligent eyewear?</strong></h2>



<p>As AI becomes more personal and integrated into everyday life, trust becomes even more important. That’s why privacy was built into intelligent eyewear from the beginning, with safeguards designed to protect users and those around them.</p>



<p>A bystander-facing LED indicator and an inner LED clearly show when recording is active. Wear detection automatically disables recording when the glasses are removed, while a thwart detection feature disables recording if the external LED indicator is blocked.</p>



<p>Users can also manage privacy and personalization settings through connected Galaxy phones. Their audio, video and screen-sharing data are not used to train AI models.</p>



<p>Samsung will continue to enhance its privacy features to give users greater transparency and control.</p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Q. What does intelligent eyewear represent for Samsung’s long-term vision?</strong></h2>



<p>Samsung has a long history of helping define new product categories — including foldables — and setting the standards consumers have come to expect from them. Intelligent eyewear is the next step in its vision to make AI more wearable, personal and seamlessly integrated into everyday life, but it is still only the beginning. With this first generation, Samsung aims to establish a new best-in-class benchmark for the emerging category and continue advancing it with each generation.</p>



<hr class="wp-block-separator has-alpha-channel-opacity" />

				<title><![CDATA[[Interview] [Galaxy Unpacked July 2026] Inside the Engineering Behind the Latest Galaxy Z Series]]></title>
				<link>https://news.samsung.com/global/interview-galaxy-unpacked-july-2026-inside-the-engineering-behind-the-latest-galaxy-z-series</link>
				<pubDate>Thu, 30 Jul 2026 10:41:00 +0000</pubDate>
								<media:content url="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/30103222/Samsung-Mobile-Galaxy-Unpacked-July-2026-Inside-the-Engineering-Interview_Thumb932_F-728x410.jpg" medium="image" />
				<dc:creator><![CDATA[Samsung Newsroom]]></dc:creator>
						<category><![CDATA[Mobile]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Foldable]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Galaxy Unpacked July 2026]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Galaxy Z Flip8]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Galaxy Z Fold8]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Galaxy Z Fold8 Ultra]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Interview]]></category>
                <guid isPermaLink="false">https://bit.ly/4c89bJu</guid>
									<description><![CDATA[On July 22 at Galaxy Unpacked July 2026, Samsung Electronics unveiled Galaxy Z Fold8 Ultra, Galaxy Z Fold8 and Galaxy Z Flip8, marking the eighth generation of the Galaxy Z series. Across seven generations, Samsung has helped define the foldable category through advances in display technology, hinge engineering and materials innovation — all driven by […]]]></description>
																<content:encoded><![CDATA[
<p>On July 22 at Galaxy Unpacked July 2026, Samsung Electronics unveiled Galaxy Z Fold8 Ultra, Galaxy Z Fold8 and Galaxy Z Flip8, marking the eighth generation of the Galaxy Z series.</p>



<p>Across seven generations, Samsung has helped define the foldable category through advances in display technology, hinge engineering and materials innovation — all driven by a deep understanding of how people use their devices.</p>



<p>To hear how those insights guided the engineering behind Samsung’s latest foldables, Samsung Newsroom sat down with Sunghoon Moon, Executive Vice President and Senior Executive of Mobile R&D Office – Hardware, Mobile eXperience (MX) Business at Samsung Electronics.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="910" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/30095534/Samsung-Mobile-Galaxy-Unpacked-July-2026-Inside-the-Engineering-Interview_main1_F.jpg" alt="Sunghoon Moon" class="wp-image-176059" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ Sunghoon Moon</figcaption></figure>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Q. How have seven generations of user insights shaped Samsung’s approach to the latest Galaxy Z series?</strong></h2>



<p>From the beginning, we saw foldables as an opportunity to redefine how people interact with their smartphones — not simply as a new form factor. Through seven generations, we’ve learned how people open, carry, unfold and rely on their devices. Those insights have shaped our engineering priorities and guided each new generation of the Galaxy Z series.</p>



<p>Over time, three priorities became clear: <strong>larger, more immersive</strong> displays; <strong>thinner and lighter</strong> devices without compromising durability; and <strong>ultra performance</strong> while preserving the unique advantages of the foldable form factor. Delivering all three became the defining engineering challenge behind <a href="https://news.samsung.com/global/samsung-galaxy-z-fold8-ultra-fold8-and-flip8foldables-perfected-for-every-way-of-living" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">the latest Galaxy Z series</a>.</p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Q.</strong> <strong>Samsung describes foldable innovation as engineering one connected system. What does that mean in practice?</strong></h2>



<p>With foldables, every component is interconnected. Making a device thinner, for example, means redesigning the display, hinge, battery, thermal system and internal architecture together. Rather than optimizing individual parts separately, we engineer the entire device as one connected system. This end-to-end engineering philosophy extends from materials and component design to manufacturing, validation and the final product experience — allowing engineering advancements to become improvements people experience every day.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="562" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/30095709/Samsung-Mobile-Galaxy-Unpacked-July-2026-Inside-the-Engineering-Interview_main2_F.jpg" alt="Samsung engineered each of the latest foldables as one connected system to bring about key improvements for users." class="wp-image-176061" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ Samsung engineered each of the latest foldables as one connected system to bring about key improvements for users.</figcaption></figure>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Q.</strong> <strong>The new Galaxy Z series introduces Flex Titanium technology. What engineering challenges did it solve?</strong></h2>



<p>Our goal was to make foldables thinner and lighter without compromising durability or the viewing experience. That required us to rework the display support structure protecting the ultra-thin OLED panel while preserving the flexibility needed for repeated folding.</p>



<p>Used in demanding industries such as aerospace, titanium offered the strength and resistance against permanent deformation essential for a foldable display. But selecting the material was only the beginning. The real challenge was developing the processing, structural design and manufacturing expertise needed to make titanium suitable for repeated folding.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="562" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/30095903/Samsung-Mobile-Galaxy-Unpacked-July-2026-Inside-the-Engineering-Interview_main3_F.jpg" alt="Flex Titanium technology makes the display thinner and more durable while reducing crease visibility." class="wp-image-176062" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ Flex Titanium technology makes the display thinner and more durable while reducing crease visibility.</figcaption></figure>



<p>Flex Titanium combines two titanium-based elements. <strong>The titanium plate</strong> beneath the display serves as the primary structural support, helping absorb pressure and impacts from both the front and rear of the device. <strong>A precision-engineered lattice structure</strong> — created through a hybrid manufacturing process — allows the plate to remain flexible while maintaining structural strength. Beneath the display panel, <strong>the titanium-alloy film</strong> provides the strength, flexibility and elastic recovery needed to support repeated everyday folding, even at a thickness of just tens of micrometers — roughly one-third the thickness of a human hair.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="562" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/30100034/Samsung-Mobile-Galaxy-Unpacked-July-2026-Inside-the-Engineering-Interview_main4_F.jpg" alt="Flex Titanium technology features an exceptionally thin titanium-alloy film and precision-engineered titanium plate" class="wp-image-176063" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ Flex Titanium technology features an exceptionally thin titanium-alloy film and precision-engineered titanium plate.</figcaption></figure>



<p>Together, these innovations strengthened the display support structure while reducing display module <strong>thickness by 10%</strong>, creating additional space for the battery, thermal system and camera. For users, that translates into a thinner, lighter device with a flatter display and reduced crease visibility.</p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Q.</strong> <strong>How did Samsung reinvent the battery system for the latest Galaxy Z series?</strong></h2>



<p>Users expect longer battery life, faster charging and a thinner foldable — without compromising safety, stability or long-term reliability. Meeting those expectations required reengineering the battery system, not simply increasing battery capacity.</p>



<p>We introduced <strong>a silicon-carbon anode</strong> to improve energy density, but the innovation extends beyond the anode. Because silicon-carbon materials are more reactive, maintaining long-term stability under high-voltage conditions meant optimizing the entire battery system. Improvements to the display structure also created additional internal space, allowing us to redesign the battery system while preserving a slimmer form factor. As a result, Galaxy Z Fold8 Ultra features <strong>a 5,000 mAh battery</strong>.</p>



<p>The same philosophy shaped charging. Rather than applying a single solution across the lineup, we developed charging architectures tailored to each foldable form factor. Galaxy Z Fold8 Ultra and Galaxy Z Fold8 use a <strong>dual-path charging architecture</strong> that helps distribute power more efficiently across the battery system, enabling faster, more stable and reliable charging while improving heat management. This supports <strong>45 W fast charging</strong>, enabling Galaxy Z Fold8 Ultra to reach a <strong>67% charge in 30 minutes</strong>. For Galaxy Z Flip8, the charging architecture was separately optimized for the unique spatial and thermal requirements of its compact form factor.</p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Q.</strong> <strong>To ensure foldables can withstand years of everyday use, how does Samsung validate durability before bringing a device to market?</strong></h2>



<p>For us, durability isn’t just about design — it must be proven through testing. We validated the complete foldable system under conditions that reflect how people use their devices. The latest Galaxy Z series was subjected to more than <strong>100 reliability validation tests</strong> covering real-world conditions including drops, repeated folding, pressure, moisture and debris. Our goal was to understand how the complete device performs in everyday use, identify potential issues before they reach customers and build foldables people can trust.</p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Q.</strong> <strong>Looking back at the entire development process, how would you describe Samsung’s approach behind the Galaxy Z series?</strong></h2>



<p>Everything starts with the user. Over seven generations, we’ve transformed user insights into innovations in materials, components and the overall device experience. That philosophy continues to guide the Galaxy Z series — delivering foldables that are thinner, more durable, more capable and more intuitive year after year.</p>

				<title><![CDATA[Samsung Electronics Announces Second Quarter 2026 Results]]></title>
				<link>https://news.samsung.com/global/samsung-electronics-announces-second-quarter-2026-results</link>
				<pubDate>Thu, 30 Jul 2026 08:56:00 +0000</pubDate>
								<media:content url="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/29111731/Samsung-Corporate-More-Stories-Second-Quarter-2026-Reults_Thumb932-728x410.jpg" medium="image" />
				<dc:creator><![CDATA[Samsung Newsroom]]></dc:creator>
						<category><![CDATA[More Stories]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Press Releases]]></category>
                <guid isPermaLink="false">https://bit.ly/44L9PZy</guid>
									<description><![CDATA[Samsung Electronics today reported financial results for the second quarter ended June 30, 2026. The Company posted KRW 171.5 trillion in consolidated revenue, another all-time quarterly high, representing a quarter-on-quarter (QoQ) increase of 28%. Operating profit also reached another all-time high, increasing to KRW 89.5 trillion. Earnings per share for both common and preferred shares […]]]></description>
																<content:encoded><![CDATA[
<p>Samsung Electronics today reported financial results for the second quarter ended June 30, 2026.</p>



<p>The Company posted KRW 171.5 trillion in consolidated revenue, another all-time quarterly high, representing a quarter-on-quarter (QoQ) increase of 28%. Operating profit also reached another all-time high, increasing to KRW 89.5 trillion. Earnings per share for both common and preferred shares increased by 52% to KRW 10,849, among the highest levels for global tech companies.</p>



<p>The Device Solutions (DS) Division posted a QoQ sales increase of 56%, with the Memory Business setting an all-time high for quarterly revenue and operating profit.</p>



<p>The Device eXperience (DX) Division posted a QoQ sales decrease of 9%.</p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>DS To Maintain Growth Momentum While Advancing Technology Leadership</strong></h2>



<p>The DS Division posted KRW 127.5 trillion in consolidated revenue and KRW 89.2 trillion in operating profit for the second quarter.</p>



<p>The Memory Business achieved another record-breaking quarter by proactively addressing AI demand despite limited capacity with a primary focus on server products. The continued industry-wide upward trend of prices also contributed to the record earnings.</p>



<p>Additionally, the Business solidified market competitiveness via industry-leading products, with server revenue achieving a record-high share of the sales mix. The Business scaled up HBM4 sales with industry-leading performance and further strengthened technology competitiveness by shipping the industry’s first HBM4E samples to major customers.</p>



<p>In H2 2026, the Memory Business expects robust demand centered on servers stemming from continued AI infrastructure capex and broader adoption of agentic AI. Meanwhile, growth in demand for server DRAM, eSSDs, and HBM is expected to accelerate. This is projected to keep the market undersupplied, despite partial demand moderation in mobile and PCs.</p>



<p>The Memory Business also plans to continue setting the pace for the market via technology leadership, seeking to optimize its portfolio in consideration of changes in demand by application and according to customer feedback. Despite efforts to increase production, supply constraints are expected to continue.</p>



<p>The Business also plans to lead the market by addressing strong demand with a focus on high-value-added products, such as HBM4, DDR5, SOCAMM2 and others, while extending its leadership into next-generation AI platforms. This follows the industry’s first HBM4E sample shipment, as well as the H1 2026 market penetration of PCIe Gen6 and UFS 5.0 solutions.</p>



<p>The System LSI Business was impacted by flagship seasonality and soft mobile demand in China, yet managed to sustain quarterly revenue through expanded sales of high-volume mobile SoCs and image sensors, driving first-half revenue to a record high. It secured orders for a next-generation flagship SoC and continued to build momentum on the back of new business from major customers.<br><br>In H2 2026, the Business expects rising component costs and soft consumer demand to persist. However, it will continue to drive sales of next-generation flagship SoCs while seeking new business for custom SoCs, while also aiming to strengthen its competitiveness in sensors and LSI products.</p>



<p>Earnings for the Foundry Business improved significantly prior to incentive-related provisions, driven by HBM base-die demand and strong orders from customers in the U.S. Design wins with major customers continued to expand, with 2nm HPC engagements as a notable example.</p>



<p>Looking ahead to H2 2026, the Foundry Business plans to ramp up production of new mobile products based on the second-generation 2nm process and expand sales of LPU and base-die products based on the 4nm process. Amid rising demand across all process nodes from customers in the U.S. and China, the Business is targeting double-digit revenue growth. Additionally, the Business will deliver mid-to-long-term growth via advanced nodes and the expansion of AI and HPC design wins.</p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>SDC To Drive Growth Through Differentiated Display Technologies</strong></h2>



<p>Samsung Display Corporation (SDC) posted KRW 7.5 trillion in consolidated revenue and KRW 0.7 trillion in operating profit for the second quarter.</p>



<p>For the small and medium display business, SDC reported an improvement in earnings based on solid demand for high-end mobile OLED products, while its large display business expanded sales volume and revenue on the growth of the gaming monitor market.</p>



<p>In H2 2026, SDC plans to focus on addressing premium smartphone demand with low-power products and diversified form factors. It will also pursue revenue growth through timely mass production of the 8.6G IT OLED line, as it seeks to expand supply of differentiated products to the tablet, gaming, and automotive markets. The large display business will focus on driving sales growth with an expanded gaming monitor customer base and strengthened product lineup.</p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>MX To Expand Flagship-Led Sales With Competitiveness of New Products</strong></h2>



<p>The MX and Networks Businesses posted KRW 33.2 trillion in consolidated revenue and reported an operating loss of KRW 0.7 trillion for the second quarter. While revenue grew year-on-year (YoY) on solid sales of the Galaxy S26 series and strong Galaxy A series momentum, earnings declined due to elevated component cost pressures across the industry.</p>



<p>In H2 2026, the MX Business plans to strengthen AI leadership through personalized and intuitive experiences and drive overall market share growth based on flagship-first expansion centered on the new Galaxy Z8 and S26 series. The Business will also seek to enhance its premium mix across the Galaxy ecosystem and deliver AI-driven experiences in new form factors with the launch of its Intelligent Eyewear. Additionally, it will pursue efficiency initiatives to mitigate the impact of rising costs.</p>



<p>In Q2 2026, the Networks Business reported an improvement in earnings QoQ and YoY, supported by overseas sales expansion. In H2 2026, the Business aims to secure profitability through new orders and structural cost discipline amid telco investment headwinds.</p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>CE Business and Harman To Further Differentiate Product Offerings Through Innovation</strong></h2>



<p>The Visual Display (VD) and Digital Appliances (DA) Businesses posted KRW 14.5 trillion in consolidated revenue, and recorded a slight operating loss for the second quarter.</p>



<p>The VD Business saw revenue and earnings increase YoY as a result of high demand from major sporting events. However, earnings declined QoQ due to increased costs.</p>



<p>In H2 2026, the VD Business will lead the AI TV market with differentiated customer experiences based on Vision AI while capturing seasonal demand through strengthened channel partnerships. The Business will also enhance TV market leadership and profitability by growing its service business through the diversification of its Samsung TV Plus content and expansion of its advertising business.</p>



<p>Meanwhile, the DA Business experienced revenue growth from increased air conditioner demand, despite cost pressures weighing on earnings. In H2 2026, the Business aims to strengthen market leadership through the expansion of AI-based product sales while pursuing profitability-driven channel diversification and enhanced product competitiveness.</p>



<p>Harman posted improved earnings on expanded automotive segment sales, as well as strong audio sales led by portable products. In H2 2026, it will seek to address demand in high-growth automotive segments, including the central compute unit. It will also expand audio sales based on an enhanced lineup and brand awareness, while maintaining stable growth momentum through revenue expansion across the automotive and audio businesses.</p>
]]></content:encoded>

																<content:encoded><![CDATA[
<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="563" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/03090642/Samsung-Worldwide-Article-Galaxy-Z-Fold8-Skydiving-Performance_Main1.jpg" alt="A team of professional skydivers formed the phone's unfolded shape." class="wp-image-176101" /></figure>



<p>To promote the launch of Galaxy Z Fold8, Samsung Gulf Electronics recently teamed up with Skydive Dubai to perform a bold aerial display, in which a team of professional skydivers formed the phone’s unfolded shape thousands of feet above the Palm Jumeirah in Dubai, one of the UAE’s premier skydiving landmarks.</p>



<p>Weighing just 201 grams, Galaxy Z Fold8, is the lightest Galaxy Z Fold device to date, making it small enough to easily fit in the pocket of a skydiving suit. The new design offers a 10:16 cover screen for everyday use and a 4:3 main screen that provides a larger, richer experience when viewing, reading and playing games.</p>



<p>The skydivers wore Galaxy Watch Ultra2 and Galaxy Watch9, to synchronize their timing and maintain their positions with extreme precision. Galaxy Watch Ultra2, in particular, is designed to deliver reliable performance even in the harshest environments and conditions. It brings an IP69K rating, a titanium body for enhanced durability, and a display with a brightness of up to 5,000 nits, ensuring visibility even during skydiving.</p>



<p>Emirati athlete and XDubai paratrooper Ahmed Al Shehhi joined the performance, documenting the experience from the ground through to its execution. During the final jump, Al Shehhi accompanied the skydiving team as they exited the aircraft, capturing additional footage and documenting a unique perspective of the Galaxy Z Fold8-inspired formation as it came together in the sky.</p>



<p>On a wider level, the aerial display symbolized the fundamental innovation of Galaxy Z Fold8. Building on seven generations of Samsung’s foldable engineering, the new form factor is intended to enhance how users today create, consume and interact with content.</p>

				<title><![CDATA[Samsung Electronics and Broadcom Expand Strategic Collaboration Across Memory and Foundry Technologies]]></title>
				<link>https://news.samsung.com/global/samsung-electronics-and-broadcom-expand-strategic-collaboration-across-memory-and-foundry-technologies</link>
				<pubDate>Sat, 25 Jul 2026 14:32:00 +0000</pubDate>
								<media:content url="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/25142854/Samsung-Semiconductors-Memory-and-Foundry-Technologies-Broadcom-MoU_Thumb932_F-728x410.jpg" medium="image" />
				<dc:creator><![CDATA[Samsung Newsroom]]></dc:creator>
						<category><![CDATA[Press Releases]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Semiconductors]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Broadcom]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[HBM]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[MoU]]></category>
                <guid isPermaLink="false">https://bit.ly/3RgydPH</guid>
									<description><![CDATA[Samsung Electronics and Broadcom today announced the signing of a memorandum of understanding (MOU) to expand their strategic collaboration across memory and foundry technologies, supporting the next generation of AI infrastructure. The MOU was announced during the AI Summit, held at The Midway in San Francisco. Attendees included Jinman Han, President and Head of Samsung […]]]></description>
																<content:encoded><![CDATA[
<p>Samsung Electronics and Broadcom today announced the signing of a memorandum of understanding (MOU) to expand their strategic collaboration across memory and foundry technologies, supporting the next generation of AI infrastructure.</p>



<p>The MOU was announced during the AI Summit, held at The Midway in San Francisco. Attendees included Jinman Han, President and Head of Samsung Electronics’ Foundry Business, Hock Tan, President and CEO of Broadcom, senior executives from both companies and representatives of the Korean government.</p>



<p>The companies expect the collaboration to be estimated at more than $200 billion across memory and foundry over the next five years through 2030.</p>



<p>On the memory front, Samsung and Broadcom plan to pursue a strategic collaboration for the supply of industry-leading memory solutions, including High Bandwidth Memory (HBM), supporting Broadcom’s next-generation AI accelerators.</p>



<p>In foundry, the companies’ collaboration focuses on Samsung’s 2-nanometer (nm) and below process technologies for Broadcom’s products, including Wireless Broadband Communications (WBC) solutions. The collaboration is expected to extend to advanced packaging technologies built on Samsung’s 2nm process, including 2.3D and 2.5D integration, to enable higher-performance and more power-efficient AI and networking silicon.</p>



<p>“AI is driving unprecedented demand for tightly integrated semiconductor technologies spanning memory, logic and advanced packaging,” said Young Hyun Jun, Vice Chairman & CEO of the Device Solutions (DS) Division at Samsung Electronics. “By expanding our collaboration with Broadcom across these critical technologies, we look forward to delivering greater value to customers while advancing the AI infrastructure of the future.”</p>



<p>“As AI infrastructure continues to scale, close collaboration across the semiconductor ecosystem becomes increasingly important,” said Charlie Kawwas, President, Semiconductor Solutions Group of Broadcom. “By combining Samsung’s memory and foundry expertise with Broadcom’s AI and connectivity leadership, we aim to continue to deliver technologies that power the next generation of AI infrastructure.”</p>



<p>With capabilities encompassing memory, logic, foundry and advanced packaging, Samsung is uniquely positioned to deliver integrated semiconductor solutions for the AI era. The expanded collaboration with Broadcom reflects Samsung’s continued focus on supporting customers with end-to-end semiconductor technologies across an increasingly diverse range of AI and high-performance computing applications.</p>
]]></content:encoded>

<p>On July 22, Samsung Electronics hosted Galaxy Unpacked July 2026 at Old Billingsgate in London — unveiling its next-generation foldables with agentic AI.</p>



<p>Alongside Galaxy Z Fold8 Ultra, Galaxy Z Fold8 and Galaxy Z Flip8, Samsung introduced Galaxy Watch Ultra2 and Galaxy Watch9 with more advanced health features, as well as intelligent eyewear. Through a user-centered Galaxy ecosystem powered by AI, the company outlined its vision for delivering meaningful everyday experiences.</p>



<p>Samsung Newsroom recaps key moments from Galaxy Unpacked July 2026.</p>

					<item>
				<title><![CDATA[[Galaxy Unpacked July 2026] Samsung and Partners Envision a Connected Care Future — From First Signal to Lasting Change]]></title>
				<link>https://news.samsung.com/global/galaxy-unpacked-july-2026-samsung-and-partners-envision-a-connected-care-future-from-first-signal-to-lasting-change</link>
				<pubDate>Thu, 23 Jul 2026 16:00:00 +0000</pubDate>
								<media:content url="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/23144743/Samsung-Mobile-Samsung-Health-Health-Forum-Sketch_Thumb932-728x410.jpg" medium="image" />
				<dc:creator><![CDATA[Samsung Newsroom]]></dc:creator>
						<category><![CDATA[Mobile]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Galaxy Unpacked]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Galaxy Unpacked July 2026]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Samsung Health]]></category>
                <guid isPermaLink="false">https://bit.ly/4pCRG9U</guid>
									<description><![CDATA[At Galaxy Unpacked July 2026 in London, Samsung Electronics set out its vision for the next chapter of digital health — a future where care moves from reactive treatment toward preventive, personalized and connected experiences, supported by trusted health innovation and partnerships across the healthcare ecosystem. To explore that vision, Samsung hosted a Health Forum […]]]></description>
																<content:encoded><![CDATA[
<p>At Galaxy Unpacked July 2026 in London, Samsung Electronics set out its vision for the next chapter of digital health — a future where care moves from reactive treatment toward preventive, personalized and connected experiences, supported by trusted health innovation and partnerships across the healthcare ecosystem.</p>



<p>To explore that vision, Samsung hosted a Health Forum panel, “Rewriting the Rules of Preventive Health.” Moderated by Dr. Hon Pak, Senior Vice President and Head of the Digital Health Team, Mobile eXperience (MX) Business at Samsung Electronics, the session brought together leaders across nutrition, fitness, clinical research and AI to examine how technology can close the gap between knowing and doing in everyday health.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="750" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/23145616/Samsung-Mobile-Samsung-Health-Health-Forum-Sketch_main1.jpg" alt="(From left) Otávio Penatti, Kevin Duffy, Moderator Dr. Hon Pak. Rohit Ail and Kartik Poria" class="wp-image-175876" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ (From left) Otávio Penatti, Kevin Duffy, Moderator Dr. Hon Pak. Rohit Ail and Kartik Poria</figcaption></figure>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>The Paradox — and the “Missing Miles” of Health</strong></h2>



<p>Opening the discussion, Dr. Pak described a paradox at the heart of modern health: innovation and technology today has been transformational and accelerating, yet chronic disease continues to rise around the world.</p>



<p>“We live in the most measured era in human history — billions of people track their sleep, their stress, their heart rate every single day. And yet chronic disease keeps rising. More data, it turns out, hasn’t resulted in better health.”</p>



<p>He framed the disconnect as two “missing miles.” The first mile is detection and monitoring: much of what shapes our health happens outside the clinic and until recently, continuous monitoring was neither available nor connected to the physician. The last mile is action: even after a diagnosis and a care plan, it is the individual who has to act day after day — and life is busy and change is hard.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="750" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/23145641/Samsung-Mobile-Samsung-Health-Health-Forum-Sketch_main2.jpg" alt="Samsung, Dr. Pak argued, is uniquely positioned to close both gaps." class="wp-image-175877" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ Dr. Hon Pak</figcaption></figure>



<p>Samsung, Dr. Pak argued, is uniquely positioned to close both gaps. With technology that stays with people throughout their daily lives — across wearables, mobile phones and a connected ecosystem — Samsung can understand not just raw biometric numbers, but the context behind them.</p>



<p>“We don’t just see a number — we see what’s around it: whether this is a moment you’re ready to change something or a moment you need a bit of support. Being there, at the point where personal decisions actually get made, is hard for a hospital or any other company to measure or act upon.”</p>



<p>But no single company can do this alone. That, he said, is why Samsung is building Connected Care together with partners — closing the first and last mile to scale prevention, with AI as the enabler across both. He introduced the three focus areas that would structure the conversation: everyday behavior, clinical credibility and privacy and advanced AI and research.</p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Everyday Behavior: Closing the Last Mile at the Dinner Table and the Gym</strong></h2>



<p>Diet and exercise are two of the biggest levers on long-term health — and two of the most within our control. They are also, Dr. Pak noted, where the gap between knowing and doing is widest.</p>



<p>Kartik Poria, Head of Product at Samsung Food, located that gap in a moment everyone recognizes.</p>



<p>“It’s Tuesday, six o’clock. You’re tired, you’re staring into the fridge and everything you know about eating well loses to whatever’s quickest. That moment is what we’re going after — because what you eat, day after day, is one of the biggest levers we have on preventing chronic disease.”</p>



<p>He described how Samsung Health and Samsung Food are coming together to tackle two halves of the same problem: Samsung Health surfaces what is happening beneath the surface — indicators such as meal logs, BMI, the Antioxidant Index and the AGEs Index — while Samsung Food’s global recipe library facilitates meal planning and its food intelligence reflect how people actually like to eat. Sitting on top is a nutrition experience grounded in behavior change science rather than tracking alone — learning a person’s habits and working with them so the healthier pattern becomes the easier one.</p>



<p>“We’re not adding another thing to manage — we’re making the healthy choice the easy one, for the night you’ve got the energy to cook and the night you don’t.”</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="750" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/23145830/Samsung-Mobile-Samsung-Health-Health-Forum-Sketch_main3.jpg" alt="Kartik Poria, Head of Product at Samsung Food, located that gap in a moment everyone recognizes." class="wp-image-175880" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ Kartik Poria from Samsung Food</figcaption></figure>



<p>Turning to movement, Kevin Duffy, CEO of iFIT, described exercise as foundational to both physical and mental wellbeing and explained how iFIT’s expert-led programs are becoming more accessible through Samsung Health.</p>



<p>“Exercise is vital for both physical and mental wellbeing — cardiovascular health, strength, mobility, sleep quality, stress management.”</p>



<p>Through Samsung Health, users can discover and subscribe to iFIT’s premium fitness programs, while iFIT workouts integrate with Galaxy Watch so users can track key metrics during guided sessions. With Fitness Index, they receive a holistic view of their physical fitness in a single, easy-to-understand score, with personalized recommendations. Looking ahead, Duffy pointed to Samsung’s Health Assistant as a way to bring a user’s Fitness Index, goals and activity history together to surface the most relevant workout at the right moment.</p>



<p>“Exercise is not just about being active — it’s about understanding your body, building sustainable habits and having the right guidance at the right time. By combining iFIT’s expert-led experiences with Samsung Health’s connected insights, we can help more people make movement a meaningful part of their everyday lives.”</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="751" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/23145858/Samsung-Mobile-Samsung-Health-Health-Forum-Sketch_main4.jpg" alt="Turning to movement, Kevin Duffy, CEO of iFIT, described exercise as foundational to both physical and mental wellbeing." class="wp-image-175881" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ Kevin Duffy from iFIT</figcaption></figure>



<p>But content and insights alone, Dr. Pak noted, don’t close the last mile. The harder question is how to move from engagement to activation. Samsung designs for real life, recognizing that people slip and miss goals most of the time — and that those moments are precisely when timely, well-contextualized support matters most.</p>



<p>The real measure, he argued, is not how often the system nudges someone, but how many healthy actions actually happen — a doctor’s low-salt advice quietly showing up as items in a grocery cart or a workout that meets a person where they actually are that day. Poria added that this is the true shift: from nudging people to changing the choice in front of them.</p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Clinical Credibility and Privacy: Trust Earned Through Evidence</strong></h2>



<p>Dr. Pak turned next to the question of trust — noting that wearable data is now something most clinicians have seen, but far fewer have been able to build into their day-to-day care. Closing that gap, he said, is a matter of evidence, workflow and trust.</p>



<p>Rohit Ail, Head of the Technology Incubation Lab at Samsung R&D Institute UK (SRUK), spoke to how his team works to generate clinical evidence in real-world settings.</p>



<p>“Our goal is to meet the standards expected by clinicians, researchers and regulatory bodies and that means generating evidence in real-world clinical environments. This commitment builds on Samsung’s growing body of clinical research and validation evidence.”</p>



<p>He referenced ongoing collaborations across Europe, including partnerships with the NHS and clinical validation studies with leading hospitals. With San Cecilio University Hospital in Spain and Gemelli in Italy, Samsung is working on identifying and managing cardiovascular disease (CVD) risk using AI and decision-support technologies; with Fondazione IRCCS in Italy, the focus is on understanding and addressing the late effects faced by adolescent and young adult (AYA) cancer survivors through AI-based digital phenotyping and a non-invasive, holistic approach.</p>



<p>On privacy, Ail noted that Samsung considers user data to belong to the user, with Samsung acting as custodian.</p>



<p>“Trust begins with protecting users’ data. Samsung takes a three-layer approach: Samsung Knox for device-level security, GDPR-compliant infrastructure and alignment with the European Health Data Space framework.”</p>



<p>He added that SRUK has also developed a Decentralized Clinical Trial framework, in which participant identities remain under the control of healthcare organizations and data is pseudonymized from the outset.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="750" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/23145939/Samsung-Mobile-Samsung-Health-Health-Forum-Sketch_main5.jpg" alt="Rohit Ail, Head of the Technology Incubation Lab at Samsung R&D Institute UK (SRUK), spoke to how his team works to generate clinical evidence in real-world settings." class="wp-image-175882" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ Rohit Ail from Samsung R&D Institute UK</figcaption></figure>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Advanced AI and Research: Reading the Body’s Signals</strong></h2>



<p>If everyday reach and clinical trust are two of the three pillars, intelligence is what binds them together. Dr. Pak introduced foundation models as a genuine turning point — a shift from population averages to something truly personal.</p>



<p>Otávio Penatti, Head of the Health AI R&D Team at Samsung R&D Institute Brazil (SRBR), explained what a foundation model for health actually means.</p>



<p>“A health foundation model is an AI model that uses self-supervised learning to learn meaningful features from unlabeled biosignal data. At Samsung, we are advancing health foundation model research across multiple global research teams, combining expertise in biosignal intelligence, wearable AI and personalized health insights. Building on the success of large language models, we can now interpret complex biosignals — such as ECG, ACC and PPG — not as isolated data points, but as a structured biological language. These signals can be measured through wearable devices in everyday situations and by applying foundation models to understand these biological patterns, we have the potential to provide more accurate predictions and continuous, personalized health insights.”</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="750" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/23150017/Samsung-Mobile-Samsung-Health-Health-Forum-Sketch_main6.jpg" alt="Otávio Penatti, Head of the Health AI R&D Team at Samsung R&D Institute Brazil (SRBR), explained what a foundation model for health actually means." class="wp-image-175883" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ Otávio Penatti from Samsung R&D Institute Brazil</figcaption></figure>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Three Focus Areas, One Goal</strong></h2>



<p>To close, Dr. Pak asked each panelist for a one-sentence vision of Connected Care in the era of AI five years from now.<strong></strong></p>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li><strong>Kartik Poria (Samsung Food):</strong> “In five years, eating well won’t run on willpower — it’ll feel like having a chef and a dietitian in your pocket, turning what your body needs into what’s for dinner, day after day.”</li>



<li><strong>Kevin Duffy (iFIT):</strong> “A coach that knows your body and adapts every workout to who you are that day.”</li>



<li><strong>Rohit Ail (SRUK):</strong> “Care that clinicians trust and patients control — evidence-based and private by design.”</li>



<li><strong>Otávio Penatti (SRBR):</strong> “AI that understands the body’s language and acts before problems begin.”</li>
</ul>



<p>Dr. Pak closed by tying the threads together: everyday behavior, clinical trust and personal AI — three focus areas, one goal of closing the first and last mile to scale prevention while meeting people where they are. “The future of health is connected, trusted and proactive — and it’s a road we’re glad to be traveling together.”</p>
]]></content:encoded>

									<description><![CDATA[Samsung Electronics has unveiled The Fold Inn, a limited-time immersive pub experience in London’s Soho neighborhood, to celebrate the launch of Galaxy Z Fold8 Ultra, Galaxy Z Fold8 and Galaxy Z Flip8 at Galaxy Unpacked July 2026 in London. Inspired by the design and versatility of Samsung’s foldable devices, the venue reimagines the traditional British pub […]]]></description>
																<content:encoded><![CDATA[
<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="667" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/28182205/Samsung-Wroldwide-Article-The-Fold-Inn-Galaxy-Z-Series_Main1.jpg" alt="Samsung Electronics has unveiled The Fold Inn, a limited-time immersive pub experience in London’s Soho neighborhood, to celebrate the launch of Galaxy Z Fold8 Ultra, Galaxy Z Fold8 and Galaxy Z Flip8 at Galaxy Unpacked July 2026 in London." class="wp-image-176040" /></figure>



<p>Samsung Electronics has unveiled The Fold Inn, a limited-time immersive pub experience in London’s Soho neighborhood, to celebrate the launch of Galaxy Z Fold8 Ultra, Galaxy Z Fold8 and Galaxy Z Flip8 at Galaxy Unpacked July 2026 in London. Inspired by the design and versatility of Samsung’s foldable devices, the venue reimagines the traditional British pub experience through the lens of foldable innovation.</p>



<p>Open to the public from July 24 to 25, The Fold Inn will feature a distinctive folded bar serving complimentary bespoke drinks inspired by Samsung’s new Galaxy Z devices. Each signature beverage reflects the personality and intended use of a different model while drawing inspiration from a well-known area of London. The experience is designed to highlight how Samsung’s foldable lineup delivers unique experiences for entertainment, productivity and self-expression, reinforcing the evolution of the Galaxy Z series over seven generations since its debut in 2019.</p>



<p>The Soho Sipper draws inspiration from Galaxy Z Fold8, catering to those interested in immersive entertainment and content discovery. Meanwhile, the City Pour showcases Galaxy Z Fold8 Ultra, built for productive multitaskers who balance work and their personal lives. Finally, the Shoreditch Pint captures the creativity, fashion and individuality that define Galaxy Z Flip8.</p>



<p>Throughout the venue, visitors will also have the opportunity to explore Samsung’s latest Galaxy Z series devices firsthand through interactive demonstrations of the newest foldable innovations. By blending hands-on product discovery with local culture, The Fold Inn uses one of London’s most recognizable social spaces to showcase the flexibility, premium design and versatility of the Galaxy Z series — and how it continues to evolve for a wide range of lifestyles and everyday needs.</p>



<p><a href="https://news.samsung.com/uk/the-fold-inn-samsung-brings-foldable-innovation-to-an-iconic-london-pub-experience" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Learn more at the Samsung U.K. Newsroom</a><a id="_msocom_1"></a></p>

								<media:content url="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/22215934/Samsung-Mobile-Galaxy-Unpacked-July-2026-Galaxy-Z-Fold8-Ultra-Galaxy-Z-Fold8-Galaxy-Z-Flip8-A-First-Look_Thumb932-728x410.jpg" medium="image" />

Combining agentic AI that understands users with hardware tailored to different lifestyles, the lineup offers three distinct foldable experiences. Samsung Newsroom got an early look at the new devices during Galaxy Unpacked July 2026 — from Galaxy Z Fold8 Ultra, […]]]></description>
																<content:encoded><![CDATA[
<p>At Galaxy Unpacked July 2026 in London, Samsung Electronics unveiled the eighth-generation Galaxy Z series. Combining agentic AI that understands users with hardware tailored to different lifestyles, the lineup offers three distinct foldable experiences.</p>



<p>Samsung Newsroom got an early look at the new devices during Galaxy Unpacked July 2026 — from Galaxy Z Fold8 Ultra, the first Ultra model in the Galaxy foldable portfolio, to Galaxy Z Fold8 with its all-new form factor and Galaxy Z Flip8 with more intuitive AI on FlexWindow.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="496" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/22220302/Samsung-Mobile-Galaxy-Unpacked-July-2026-Galaxy-Z-Fold8-Ultra-Galaxy-Z-Fold8-Galaxy-Z-Flip8-A-First-Look_main1.jpg" alt="(From left) Galaxy Z Flip8, Galaxy Z Fold8 and Galaxy Z Fold8 Ultra" class="wp-image-175788" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ (From left) Galaxy Z Flip8, Galaxy Z Fold8 and Galaxy Z Fold8 Ultra</figcaption></figure>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Galaxy Z Fold8 Ultra: The Ultra Experience on a Foldable Display</strong></h2>



<p>Within the Galaxy lineup, the “Ultra” name represents Samsung’s highest level of performance and user experience. As the first Ultra model in the Galaxy Z series, Galaxy Z Fold8 Ultra pairs ultra-level performance with an expansive foldable display to elevate productivity through AI-powered multitasking.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="564" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/22220341/Samsung-Mobile-Galaxy-Unpacked-July-2026-Galaxy-Z-Fold8-Ultra-Galaxy-Z-Fold8-Galaxy-Z-Flip8-A-First-Look_main2.jpg" alt="Galaxy Z Fold8 Ultra features a remarkably thin profile when unfolded." class="wp-image-175789" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ Galaxy Z Fold8 Ultra features a remarkably thin profile when unfolded.</figcaption></figure>



<p>The first thing that stands out is how thin the device is. Unfolding it reveals a spacious 8-inch main display. Measuring 4.1 millimeters thick when unfolded and weighing only 215 grams, Galaxy Z Fold8 Ultra is the thinnest Galaxy Z Fold yet — making it comfortable to carry and use throughout the day.</p>



<p>The display has also been refined to deliver smoother, more comfortable folding. The folding magnets, hinge mechanism and display tension have been precisely optimized, while refinements to the edge design make the device easier to unfold. Samsung’s proprietary Flex Titanium technology helps minimize the display crease while enhancing durability.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="725" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/22220359/Samsung-Mobile-Galaxy-Unpacked-July-2026-Galaxy-Z-Fold8-Ultra-Galaxy-Z-Fold8-Galaxy-Z-Flip8-A-First-Look_main3.jpg" alt="Flex Titanium technology reduces crease visibility." class="wp-image-175790" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ Flex Titanium technology reduces crease visibility.</figcaption></figure>



<p>The expansive display also unlocks enhanced AI experiences.</p>



<p>Gemini Notebook streamlines the process of organizing meeting materials and notes. Users can upload a PDF of meeting minutes or an audio recording of a presentation to generate a summary of the key points, then transform the same source material into a variety of formats — including AI-generated videos, presentation slides, podcasts and quizzes.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="747" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/22220433/Samsung-Mobile-Galaxy-Unpacked-July-2026-Galaxy-Z-Fold8-Ultra-Galaxy-Z-Fold8-Galaxy-Z-Flip8-A-First-Look_main4.jpg" alt="Gemini Notebook streamlines the process of organizing meeting materials and notes." class="wp-image-175791" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ Gemini Notebook</figcaption></figure>



<p>Galaxy Z Fold8 Ultra truly shines in AI-powered multitasking. Agentic AI anticipates when multitasking is needed, using Now Nudge to open Multi Window at the right moment. For example, Now Nudge can suggest adding dinner plans from a messaging app to the calendar and open the Calendar app in Multi Window.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="450" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/22220448/Samsung-Mobile-Galaxy-Unpacked-July-2026-Galaxy-Z-Fold8-Ultra-Galaxy-Z-Fold8-Galaxy-Z-Flip8-A-First-Look_main5.jpg" alt="Agentic AI anticipates when multitasking is needed, using Now Nudge to open Multi Window at the right moment." class="wp-image-175792" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ Now Nudge</figcaption></figure>



<p>Qualcomm Snapdragon® 8 Elite Gen 5 for Galaxy and a 5,000mAh battery support Galaxy AI experiences and seamless multitasking on the expansive display.</p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Galaxy Z Fold8: A Foldable, Reimagined</strong></h2>



<p>Redesigned with an all-new form factor, Galaxy Z Fold8 introduces an aspect ratio optimized for a wide range of content.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="667" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/22220505/Samsung-Mobile-Galaxy-Unpacked-July-2026-Galaxy-Z-Fold8-Ultra-Galaxy-Z-Fold8-Galaxy-Z-Flip8-A-First-Look_main6.jpg" alt="Galaxy Z Fold8 features new display proportions across its 5.5-inch cover screen and a 7.6-inch main display." class="wp-image-175793" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ Galaxy Z Fold8 features new display proportions across its 5.5-inch cover screen and a 7.6-inch main display.</figcaption></figure>



<p>Unfolding the device expands the display to a 4:3 aspect ratio, ideal for watching movies or playing games. Rotating the device switches to a 3:4 aspect ratio that feels more natural for reading e-books or browsing the web. The cover screen’s 10:16 aspect ratio also provides a familiar experience for browsing short-form content.</p>



<p>A 4,800mAh battery supports more than 26 hours of video playback. Weighing just 201 grams, Galaxy Z Fold8 is the lightest Galaxy Z Fold yet and remains comfortable to hold — even during extended viewing sessions.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-video"><video playsinline autoplay controls loop muted src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/22220556/Samsung-Mobile-Galaxy-Unpacked-July-2026-Galaxy-Z-Fold8-Ultra-Galaxy-Z-Fold8-Galaxy-Z-Flip8-A-First-Look_main7.mp4"></video><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ Optimized aspect ratios enhance content immersion.</figcaption></figure>



<p>Galaxy Z Fold8 goes beyond content consumption with tools for creating and editing.</p>



<p>Galaxy AI-powered My FanCam makes it easy to create videos focused on a specific person. After selecting a video in the Gallery app, users can launch My FanCam and choose the subject to follow. The video is then automatically reframed around the selected subject.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-video"><video playsinline autoplay controls loop muted src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/22220901/Samsung-Mobile-Galaxy-Unpacked-July-2026-Galaxy-Z-Fold8-Ultra-Galaxy-Z-Fold8-Galaxy-Z-Flip8-A-First-Look_main8.mp4"></video><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ My FanCam</figcaption></figure>



<p>The device also delivers a more advanced agentic AI experience.</p>



<p>For example, if a user shares a photo of the Eiffel Tower with Gemini and asks it to book a nearby hotel, the AI agent finds accommodations that match the specified criteria — even while they continue using other apps. Afterward, they can simply review the recommendations and approve only the actions that require confirmation, saving time and effort.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="387" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/22220923/Samsung-Mobile-Galaxy-Unpacked-July-2026-Galaxy-Z-Fold8-Ultra-Galaxy-Z-Fold8-Galaxy-Z-Flip8-A-First-Look_main9.jpg" alt="For example, if a user shares a photo of the Eiffel Tower with Gemini and asks it to book a nearby hotel, the AI agent finds accommodations that match the specified criteria." class="wp-image-175798" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ Agentic AI</figcaption></figure>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Galaxy Z Flip8: More Without Unfolding</strong></h2>



<p>Galaxy Z Flip8 makes a strong first impression with its thin, lightweight design. Measuring 6.1 millimeters thick when unfolded and weighing only 180 grams, it offers excellent portability. A stronger frame and a more durable hinge made with a new high-strength material improve durability while maintaining the device’s slim profile.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="726" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/22220943/Samsung-Mobile-Galaxy-Unpacked-July-2026-Galaxy-Z-Fold8-Ultra-Galaxy-Z-Fold8-Galaxy-Z-Flip8-A-First-Look_main10.jpg" alt="Galaxy Z Flip8 is comfortable to hold." class="wp-image-175800" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ Galaxy Z Flip8 is comfortable to hold.</figcaption></figure>



<p>One of Galaxy Z Flip8’s biggest upgrades is its more versatile FlexWindow. Users can check important information and access their favorite features without unfolding the device.</p>



<p>Another highlight is Galaxy AI’s Now Brief. From FlexWindow, users can instantly see the day’s weather, schedule and important notifications. Everyday information such as exchange rates and stock updates is organized into personalized cards, making it easy to stay on top of the day.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="467" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/22220958/Samsung-Mobile-Galaxy-Unpacked-July-2026-Galaxy-Z-Fold8-Ultra-Galaxy-Z-Fold8-Galaxy-Z-Flip8-A-First-Look_main11.jpg" alt="From FlexWindow, users can instantly see the day’s weather, schedule and important notifications." class="wp-image-175801" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ Now Brief on FlexWindow</figcaption></figure>



<p>App functionality also extends to FlexWindow. Without unfolding the device, users can browse photos in the Gallery app, chat with friends through messaging apps and quickly complete tasks. More than a hub for notifications and widgets, FlexWindow has evolved into a gateway to more of what Galaxy has to offer.</p>



<p>The camera experience has also been refined to better complement the Galaxy Z Flip form factor. Camcorder Grip — exclusive to the Galaxy Z Flip series — enables stable one-handed recording, while Super Steady’s horizontal lock helps keep footage smooth and level, even on the move.</p>



<p>Dual Recording captures both the creator and the subject in the same video, perfect for vlogs and social media challenges. Opening the Camera app displays the creator’s view at the top of the screen and the subject’s view below, allowing both perspectives to be recorded simultaneously. A live preview is also available on the cover screen, so both can see the shot while filming.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="649" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/22221014/Samsung-Mobile-Galaxy-Unpacked-July-2026-Galaxy-Z-Fold8-Ultra-Galaxy-Z-Fold8-Galaxy-Z-Flip8-A-First-Look_main12.jpg" alt="Dual Recording captures both the creator and the subject in the same video, perfect for vlogs and social media challenges." class="wp-image-175802" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ Dual Recording</figcaption></figure>



<p>From AI-powered productivity on a large display to optimized content viewing and everyday convenience without unfolding, the eighth-generation Galaxy Z series expands the possibilities of foldables. Designed to suit a wide range of needs, the latest Galaxy foldables offer users more ways to choose the mobile experience that suits them.</p>
